L ## Literals
T 11 text.html.markdown
L 
L Literal schemas represent a [literal type](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/everyday-types.html#literal-types), like `"hello world"` or `5`.
T 154 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const tuna = z.literal("tuna");
T 31 text.html.markdown
L const twelve = z.literal(12);
T 29 text.html.markdown
L const twobig = z.literal(2n);
T 29 text.html.markdown
L const tru = z.literal(true);
T 28 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To represent the JavaScript literals `null` and `undefined`:
T 60 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.null();
T 9 text.html.markdown
L z.undefined();
T 14 text.html.markdown
L z.void(); // equivalent to z.undefined()
T 40 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To allow multiple literal values:
T 33 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const colors = z.literal(["red", "green", "blue"]);
T 51 text.html.markdown
L 
L colors.parse("green"); // ✅
T 29 text.html.markdown
L colors.parse("yellow"); // ❌
T 30 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L ### Emails
T 10 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate email addresses:
T 28 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.email();
T 10 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, Zod uses a comparatively strict email regex designed to validate normal email addresses containing common characters. It's roughly equivalent to the rules enforced by Gmail. To learn more about this regex, refer to [this post](https://colinhacks.com/essays/reasonable-email-regex).
T 293 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L /^(?!\.)(?!.*\.\.)([a-z0-9_'+\-\.]*)[a-z0-9_+-]@([a-z0-9][a-z0-9\-]*\.)+[a-z]{2,}$/i
T 84 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To customize the email validation behavior, you can pass a custom regular expression to the `pattern` param.
T 108 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.email({ pattern: /your regex here/ });
T 40 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Zod exports several useful regexes you could use.
T 49 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L // Zod's default email regex
T 28 text.html.markdown
L z.email();
T 10 text.html.markdown
L z.email({ pattern: z.regexes.email }); // equivalent
T 52 text.html.markdown
L 
L // the regex used by browsers to validate input[type=email] fields
T 66 text.html.markdown
L // https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/input/email
T 72 text.html.markdown
L z.email({ pattern: z.regexes.html5Email });
T 43 text.html.markdown
L 
L // the classic emailregex.com regex (RFC 5322)
T 46 text.html.markdown
L z.email({ pattern: z.regexes.rfc5322Email });
T 45 text.html.markdown
L 
L // a loose regex that allows Unicode (good for intl emails)
T 59 text.html.markdown
L z.email({ pattern: z.regexes.unicodeEmail });
T 45 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### UUIDs
T 9 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate UUIDs:
T 18 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown

T 9 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To specify a particular UUID version:
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L // supports "v1", "v2", "v3", "v4", "v5", "v6", "v7", "v8"
T 58 text.html.markdown
L z.uuid({ version: "v4" });
T 26 text.html.markdown
L 
L // for convenience
T 18 text.html.markdown
L z.uuidv4();
T 11 text.html.markdown
L z.uuidv6();
T 11 text.html.markdown
L z.uuidv7();
T 11 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The RFC 9562/4122 UUID spec requires the first two bits of byte 8 to be `10`. Other UUID-like identifiers do not enforce this constraint. To validate any UUID-like identifier:
T 175 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.guid();
T 9 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ### URLs
T 8 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate any WHATWG-compatible URL:
T 38 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.url();
T 23 text.html.markdown
L 
L schema.parse("https://example.com"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("http://localhost"); // ✅
T 40 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("mailto:noreply@zod.dev"); // ✅
T 46 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L As you can see this is quite permissive. Internally this uses the `new URL()` constructor to validate inputs; this behavior may differ across platforms and runtimes but it's the mostly rigorous way to validate URIs/URLs on any given JS runtime/engine. 
T 252 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate the hostname against a specific regex:
T 50 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.url({ hostname: /^example\.com$/ });
T 53 text.html.markdown
L 
L schema.parse("https://example.com"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("https://zombo.com"); // ❌
T 41 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate the protocol against a specific regex, use the `protocol` param.
T 76 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.url({ protocol: /^https$/ });
T 46 text.html.markdown
L 
L schema.parse("https://example.com"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("http://example.com"); // ❌
T 42 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L <Callout>
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 8 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L   **Web URLs** — In many cases, you'll want to validate Web URLs specifically. Use `z.httpUrl()`:
T 99 text.html.markdown
L 
L   ```ts
T 7 text.html.markdown
L   z.httpUrl();
T 14 text.html.markdown
L 
L   // equivalent to
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   z.url({
T 9 text.html.markdown
L     protocol: /^https?$/,
T 25 text.html.markdown
L     hostname: z.regexes.domain
T 30 text.html.markdown
L   });
T 5 text.html.markdown
L   ```
T 5 text.html.markdown
L 
L   This restricts the protocol to `http`/`https` and ensures the hostname is a valid domain name with the `z.regexes.domain` regular expression:
T 143 text.html.markdown
L 
L   ```ts
T 7 text.html.markdown
L   /^(?=.{1,253}$)([a-zA-Z0-9](?:[a-zA-Z0-9-]{0,61}[a-zA-Z0-9])?\.)+[a-zA-Z]{2,63}$/
T 83 text.html.markdown
L   ```
T 5 text.html.markdown
L </Callout>
T 2 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 10 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L 
L To normalize URLs, use the `normalize` flag. This will overwrite the input value with the [normalized URL](https://chatgpt.com/share/6881547f-bebc-800f-9093-f5981e277c2c) returned by `new URL()`.
T 195 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L new URL("HTTP://ExAmPle.com:80/./a/../b?X=1#f oo").href
T 55 text.html.markdown
L // => "http://example.com/b?X=1#f%20oo"
T 39 text.html.markdown
L ```

T 5 text.html.markdown
L const phone = z.e164();
T 23 text.html.markdown
L

L phone.parse("555-555-5555"); // ❌

T 100 text.html.markdown

L ### ISO datetimes
T 17 text.html.markdown
L 
L As you may have noticed, Zod string includes a few date/time related validations. These validations are regular expression based, so they are not as strict as a full date/time library. However, they are very convenient for validating user input.
T 245 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `z.iso.datetime()` method accepts a strict subset of ISO 8601; by default, no timezone offsets are allowed:
T 111 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const datetime = z.iso.datetime();
T 34 text.html.markdown
L 
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ✅
T 46 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ✅
T 50 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123456Z"); // ✅ (arbitrary precision)
T 75 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00+02:00"); // ❌ (offsets not allowed)
T 73 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00"); // ❌ (local not allowed)
T 65 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To allow timezone offsets:
T 26 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const datetime = z.iso.datetime({ offset: true });
T 50 text.html.markdown
L 
L // allows timezone offsets
T 26 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00+02:00"); // ✅
T 51 text.html.markdown
L 
L // basic offsets not allowed
T 28 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00+02");    // ❌
T 51 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00+0200");  // ❌
T 51 text.html.markdown
L 
L // Z is still supported
T 23 text.html.markdown
L datetime.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ✅ 
T 47 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To allow unqualified (timezone-less) datetimes:
T 47 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.iso.datetime({ local: true });
T 47 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:01"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("2020-01-01T06:15"); // ✅ seconds optional
T 57 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ✅
T 44 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ❌ (a `Z` requires seconds)
T 66 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To constrain the allowable time `precision`. By default, seconds are required and sub-second precision is arbitrary. RFC 3339 mandates the seconds wherever a `Z` or an offset is present, so only the unqualified form `local` adds may omit them.
T 243 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const a = z.iso.datetime();
T 27 text.html.markdown
L a.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ❌ (seconds required)
T 55 text.html.markdown
L a.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ✅
T 39 text.html.markdown
L a.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L 
L const b = z.iso.datetime({ precision: -1 }); // minute precision (no seconds)
T 77 text.html.markdown
L b.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ✅
T 36 text.html.markdown
L b.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ❌
T 39 text.html.markdown
L b.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ❌
T 43 text.html.markdown
L 
L const c = z.iso.datetime({ precision: 0 }); // second precision only
T 68 text.html.markdown
L c.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ❌
T 36 text.html.markdown
L c.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ✅
T 39 text.html.markdown
L c.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ❌
T 43 text.html.markdown
L 
L const d = z.iso.datetime({ precision: 3 }); // millisecond precision only
T 73 text.html.markdown
L d.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ❌
T 36 text.html.markdown
L d.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00Z"); // ❌
T 39 text.html.markdown
L d.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ✅
T 43 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To accept minute precision alongside the rest, union the two — no single `precision` covers both.
T 99 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const mixed = z.union([z.iso.datetime(), z.iso.datetime({ precision: -1 })]);
T 77 text.html.markdown
L mixed.parse("2020-01-01T06:15Z"); // ✅
T 40 text.html.markdown
L mixed.parse("2020-01-01T06:15:00.123Z"); // ✅
T 47 text.html.markdown
L mixed.parse("2020-01-01T06:15"); // ❌ (still qualified-only)
T 62 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### ISO dates
T 13 text.html.markdown

T 5 text.html.markdown
L const date = z.iso.date();
T 26 text.html.markdown
L 
L date.parse("2020-01-01"); // ✅
T 32 text.html.markdown
L date.parse("2020-1-1"); // ❌
T 30 text.html.markdown
L date.parse("2020-01-32"); // ❌
T 32 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### ISO times
T 13 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `z.iso.time()` method validates strings in the format `HH:MM[:SS[.s+]]`. By default seconds are optional, as are sub-second decimals.
T 137 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const time = z.iso.time();
T 26 text.html.markdown
L 
L time.parse("03:15"); // ✅
T 27 text.html.markdown
L time.parse("03:15:00"); // ✅
T 30 text.html.markdown
L time.parse("03:15:00.9999999"); // ✅ (arbitrary precision)
T 60 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L No offsets of any kind are allowed.
T 35 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L time.parse("03:15:00Z"); // ❌ (no `Z` allowed)
T 48 text.html.markdown
L time.parse("03:15:00+02:00"); // ❌ (no offsets allowed)
T 57 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Use the `precision` parameter to constrain the allowable decimal precision.
T 75 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.iso.time({ precision: -1 }); // HH:MM (minute precision)
T 58 text.html.markdown
L z.iso.time({ precision: 0 });  // HH:MM:SS (second precision)
T 61 text.html.markdown
L z.iso.time({ precision: 1 });  // HH:MM:SS.s (decisecond precision)
T 67 text.html.markdown
L z.iso.time({ precision: 2 });  // HH:MM:SS.ss (centisecond precision)
T 69 text.html.markdown
L z.iso.time({ precision: 3 });  // HH:MM:SS.sss (millisecond precision)
T 70 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L const ipv4 = z.ipv4();

L ### MAC Addresses
T 17 text.html.markdown
L 
L Validate standard 48-bit MAC address [IEEE 802](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MAC_address).
T 91 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const mac = z.mac(); 
T 21 text.html.markdown
L mac.parse("00:1A:2B:3C:4D:5E");  // ✅
T 39 text.html.markdown
L mac.parse("00-1a-2b-3c-4d-5e");  // ❌ colon-delimited by default
T 66 text.html.markdown
L mac.parse("001A:2B3C:4D5E");     // ❌ standard formats only
T 61 text.html.markdown
L mac.parse("00:1A:2b:3C:4d:5E");  // ❌ no mixed case
T 53 text.html.markdown
L 
L // custom delimiter
T 19 text.html.markdown
L const dashMac = z.mac({ delimiter: "-" });
T 42 text.html.markdown
L dashMac.parse("00-1A-2B-3C-4D-5E"); // ✅
T 42 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ### Credit card numbers
T 23 text.html.markdown
L 
L Validate a card number: 12 to 19 digits with a valid [Luhn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhn_algorithm) checksum. The issuer is not identified, so cards from any scheme are accepted.
T 184 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const card = z.creditCard();
T 28 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111111111111111");     // ✅
T 42 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111 1111 1111 1111");  // ✅ single spaces
T 56 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111-1111-1111-1111");  // ✅ single hyphens
T 57 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111  1111 1111 1111"); // ❌ no repeated separators
T 65 text.html.markdown
L card.parse(" 4111111111111111");    // ❌ no surrounding whitespace
T 68 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111.1111.1111.1111");  // ❌ spaces and hyphens only
T 66 text.html.markdown
L card.parse("4111111111111112");     // ❌ failed checksum
T 58 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### Currency codes
T 18 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `z.currencyCode()` method validates three-letter ISO 4217 currency codes.
T 77 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const currency = z.currencyCode();
T 34 text.html.markdown
L 
L currency.parse("USD"); // ✅
T 29 text.html.markdown
L currency.parse("usd"); // ❌
T 29 text.html.markdown
L currency.parse("ANG"); // ❌ withdrawn from ISO 4217
T 53 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ### IBANs
T 9 text.html.markdown
L 
L Validate an International Bank Account Number (IBAN): 2-letter country code, 2 check digits, and 11 to 30 alphanumeric characters with a valid [ISO 7064 MOD 97-10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Bank_Account_Number#Validating_the_IBAN) checksum. The country registry is not consulted, so any country code is accepted and no country's own account length is enforced. Electronic format only.
T 402 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const iban = z.iban();
T 22 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("DE89370400440532013000");      // ✅
T 49 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("NO9386011117947");             // ✅
T 49 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00"); // ❌ no spaces
T 59 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("de89370400440532013000");      // ❌ uppercase only
T 64 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("DE89370400440532013001");      // ❌ failed checksum
T 65 text.html.markdown
L iban.parse("DE00000000000000000066");      // ❌ check digits are always 02–98
T 81 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L ### Hashes
T 10 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate cryptographic hash values:
T 38 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("md5");
T 14 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha1");
T 15 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha256");
T 17 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha384");
T 17 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha512");
T 17 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, `z.hash()` expects hexadecimal encoding, as is conventional. You can specify a different encoding with the `enc` parameter:
T 135 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha256", { enc: "hex" });       // default
T 50 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha256", { enc: "base64" });    // base64 encoding
T 58 text.html.markdown
L z.hash("sha256", { enc: "base64url" }); // base64url encoding (no padding)
T 74 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ### Custom formats
T 18 text.html.markdown
L 
L To define your own string formats:
T 34 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const coolId = z.stringFormat("cool-id", (val)=>{
T 49 text.html.markdown
L   // arbitrary validation here
T 30 text.html.markdown
L   return val.length === 100 && val.startsWith("cool-");
T 55 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L // a regex is also accepted
T 27 text.html.markdown
L z.stringFormat("cool-id", /^cool-[a-z0-9]{95}$/);
T 49 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This schema will produce `"invalid_format"` issues, which are more descriptive than the `"custom"` errors produced by refinements or `z.custom()`.
T 146 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L myFormat.parse("invalid input!");
T 33 text.html.markdown
L // ZodError: [
T 14 text.html.markdown
L //   {
T 6 text.html.markdown
L //     "code": "invalid_format",
T 32 text.html.markdown
L //     "format": "cool-id",
T 27 text.html.markdown
L //     "path": [],
T 18 text.html.markdown
L //     "message": "Invalid cool-id"
T 35 text.html.markdown
L //   }
T 6 text.html.markdown
L // ]
T 4 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ## Template literals
T 20 text.html.markdown
L 
L > Introduced in `zod@4.0`.
T 26 text.html.markdown
L 
L To define a template literal schema:
T 36 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.templateLiteral([ "hello, ", z.string(), "!" ]);
T 65 text.html.markdown
L // `hello, ${string}!`
T 22 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `z.templateLiteral` API can handle any number of string literals (e.g. `"hello"`) and schemas. Any schema with an inferred type that's assignable to `string | number | bigint | boolean | null | undefined` can be passed.
T 223 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.templateLiteral([ "hi there" ]);
T 34 text.html.markdown
L // `hi there`
T 13 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.templateLiteral([ "email: ", z.string() ]);
T 45 text.html.markdown
L // `email: ${string}`
T 21 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.templateLiteral([ "high", z.literal(5) ]);
T 44 text.html.markdown
L // `high5`
T 10 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.templateLiteral([ z.nullable(z.literal("grassy")) ]);
T 55 text.html.markdown
L // `grassy` | `null`
T 20 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.templateLiteral([ z.number(), z.enum(["px", "em", "rem"]) ]);
T 63 text.html.markdown
L // `${number}px` | `${number}em` | `${number}rem`
T 49 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

T 38 text.html.markdown
L ```

L ## Stringbools [#stringbool]
T 28 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L > Introduced in `zod@4.0`.
T 26 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L In some cases (e.g. parsing environment variables) it's valuable to parse certain string "boolish" values to a plain `boolean` value. Use `z.stringbool()` for this:
T 164 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L const strbool = z.stringbool();
T 31 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L strbool.parse("true")         // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("1")            // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("yes")          // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("on")           // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("y")            // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("enabled")      // => true
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L strbool.parse("false");       // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("0");           // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("no");          // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("off");         // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("n");           // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L strbool.parse("disabled");    // => false
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L 
L strbool.parse(/* anything else */); // ZodError<[{ code: "invalid_value" }]>
T 76 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown markup.fenced_code.block.markdown punctuation.definition.markdown

L ## Unknown
T 10 text.html.markdown
L 
L Zod aims to mirror TypeScript's type system one-to-one. As such, Zod provides APIs to represent the following special types:
T 124 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L // allows any values
T 20 text.html.markdown
L z.any(); // inferred type: `any`
T 32 text.html.markdown
L z.unknown(); // inferred type: `unknown`
T 40 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L As object properties, their keys are required, matching `{ a: any }` in TypeScript.
T 83 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.any() }).parse({}); // ❌
T 42 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.any() }).parse({ a: undefined }); // ✅
T 56 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.any().optional() }).parse({}); // ✅
T 53 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ## Never
T 8 text.html.markdown
L 
L No value will pass validation.
T 30 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.never(); // inferred type: `never`
T 36 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

T 20 text.html.markdown
L

T 77 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts z.object
T 14 text.html.markdown
L const StrictDog = z.strictObject({
T 34 text.html.markdown
L   name: z.string(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L });

L

T 52 text.html.markdown
L // ❌ throws
T 13 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### `z.looseObject`
T 19 text.html.markdown
L

T 68 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts z.object
T 14 text.html.markdown

T 32 text.html.markdown
L   name: z.string(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

T 51 text.html.markdown

T 40 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L This API can be used to overwrite existing fields! Be careful with this power! If the two schemas share keys, B will override A.
T 128 text.html.markdown
L 
L <Callout>
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 8 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L **Alternative: spread syntax** — You can alternatively avoid `.extend()` altogether by creating a new object schema entirely. This makes the strictness level of the resulting schema visually obvious.
T 201 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const DogWithBreed = z.object({ // or z.strictObject() or z.looseObject()...
T 76 text.html.markdown
L   ...Dog.shape,
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   breed: z.string(),
T 20 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L You can also use this to merge multiple objects in one go.
T 58 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const DogWithBreed = z.object({
T 31 text.html.markdown
L   ...Animal.shape,
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   ...Pet.shape,
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   breed: z.string(),
T 20 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This approach has a few advantages:
T 35 text.html.markdown
L 
L 1. It uses language-level features ([spread syntax](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Operators/Spread_syntax)) instead of library-specific APIs
T 176 text.html.markdown
L 2. The same syntax works in Zod and Zod Mini
T 44 text.html.markdown
L 3. It's more `tsc`-efficient — the `.extend()` method can be expensive on large schemas, and due to [a TypeScript limitation](https://github.com/microsoft/TypeScript/pull/61505) it gets quadratically more expensive when calls are chained
T 239 text.html.markdown
L 4. If you wish, you can change the strictness level of the resulting schema by using `z.strictObject()` or `z.looseObject()`
T 124 text.html.markdown
L </Callout>
T 2 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 10 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html

L ### `.safeExtend()`
T 19 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `.safeExtend()` method works similarly to `.extend()`, but it won't let you overwrite an existing property with a non-assignable schema. In other words, the result of `.safeExtend()` will have an inferred type that [`extends`](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/conditional-types.html#conditional-type-constraints) the original (in the TypeScript sense).
T 370 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.string() }).safeExtend({ a: z.string().min(5) }); // ✅
T 72 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.string() }).safeExtend({ a: z.any() }); // ✅
T 62 text.html.markdown
L z.object({ a: z.string() }).safeExtend({ a: z.number() });
T 58 text.html.markdown
L //                                       ^  ❌ ZodNumber is not assignable 
T 76 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Use `.safeExtend()` to extend schemas that contain refinements. (Regular `.extend()` will throw an error when used on schemas with refinements.)
T 144 text.html.markdown
L 
L <Tabs groupId="lib" items={["Zod", "Zod Mini"]}>

L ### `z.deepPartial()` [#deep-partial]
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L Where `.partial()` only touches the top-level shape, `z.deepPartial()` recurses to every object in the tree — through arrays, tuples, unions, records, and wrappers.
T 166 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Post = z.object({
T 23 text.html.markdown
L   title: z.string(),
T 20 text.html.markdown
L   author: z.object({ name: z.string(), email: z.string() }),
T 60 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.deepPartial(Post).parse({ author: {} }); // ✅
T 49 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The source schema is never modified, and the result is still a `ZodObject`, so `.shape` and `.extend()` keep working. A [discriminated union](#discriminated-unions) degrades to a plain `z.union()`, since an optional discriminator defeats the discriminated lookup. Like `.partial()`, it throws on an object carrying its own [refinement](#refinements).
T 350 text.html.markdown
L

L ### Symbol keys
T 15 text.html.markdown
L 
L A shape can declare a symbol key. A `const` symbol infers as `unique symbol`, so the key is tracked in the inferred type and its value is validated like any other:
T 163 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const TAG = Symbol("tag");
T 26 text.html.markdown
L const Post = z.object({ title: z.string(), [TAG]: z.number() });
T 64 text.html.markdown
L // { title: string; [TAG]: number }
T 35 text.html.markdown
L 
L Post.parse({ title: "hello", [TAG]: 42 }); // ✅
T 49 text.html.markdown
L Post.parse({ title: "hello" });            // ❌ the symbol key is required
T 76 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Declared symbol keys work with `.partial()`, `.pick()`, `.omit()`, `.extend()`, and the rest. Symbol keys the shape doesn't declare are ignored — `z.looseObject()` won't pass them through, and `z.strictObject()` won't flag them.
T 230 text.html.markdown
L 
L ## Recursive objects
T 20 text.html.markdown
L 
L To define a self-referential type, use a [getter](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Functions/get) on the key. This lets JavaScript resolve the cyclical schema at runtime.
T 203 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Category = z.object({
T 27 text.html.markdown
L   name: z.string(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   get subcategories(){
T 22 text.html.markdown
L     return z.array(Category)
T 28 text.html.markdown
L   }
T 3 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L type Category = z.infer<typeof Category>;
T 23 text.html.markdown
T 24 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.Category.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 40 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 41 text.html.markdown
L // { name: string; subcategories: Category[] }
T 46 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Cyclical inputs work out of the box with Zod. For bundle size reasons, Zod Mini requires you to register a memoizer explicitly (see code example).

T 23 text.html.markdown

T 3 text.html.markdown

L All object APIs (`.pick()`, `.omit()`, `.required()`, `.partial()`, etc.) work as you'd expect.
T 95 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### Circularity errors
T 22 text.html.markdown
L 
L Due to TypeScript limitations, recursive type inference can be finicky, and it only works in certain scenarios. Some more complicated types may trigger recursive type errors like this:
T 184 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Activity = z.object({
T 27 text.html.markdown
L   name: z.string(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   get subactivities() {
T 23 text.html.markdown
L     // ^ ❌ 'subactivities' implicitly has return type 'any' because it does not
T 81 text.html.markdown
L     // have a return type annotation and is referenced directly or indirectly
T 77 text.html.markdown
L     // in one of its return expressions.ts(7023)
T 48 text.html.markdown
L 
L     return z.nullable(z.array(Activity));
T 41 text.html.markdown
L   },
T 4 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L In these cases, you can resolve the error with a type annotation on the offending getter:
T 89 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Activity = z.object({
T 27 text.html.markdown
L   name: z.string(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   get subactivities(): z.ZodNullable<z.ZodArray<typeof Activity>> {
T 36 text.html.markdown
T 37 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 47 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 54 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative invalid.illegal.character-not-allowed-here.html
T 55 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 63 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.Activity.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 64 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 67 text.html.markdown
L     return z.nullable(z.array(Activity));
T 41 text.html.markdown
L   },
T 4 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L {/* Some general rules of thumb for avoiding circularity  */}
T 61 text.html.markdown
L 
L {/* 
T 4 text.html.markdown
L <Accordions>
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html

T 2 text.html.markdown
T 3 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 12 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 13 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative

T 20 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.begin.html
T 62 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html
T 63 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.end.html
T 64 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L     Recursive type inference can by mysterious. TypeScript is capable of it in certain limited scenarios. Depending on what you're trying to do, you may encounter errors like this:
T 180 text.html.markdown
L 
L     ```ts
T 9 text.html.markdown
L     export const Activity = z.object({
T 38 text.html.markdown
L       name: z.string(),
T 23 text.html.markdown
L       get children() {
T 22 text.html.markdown
L         // ^ ❌ 'children' implicitly has return type 'any' because it does not 
T 81 text.html.markdown
L         // have a return type annotation and is referenced directly or indirectly 
T 82 text.html.markdown
L         // in one of its return expressions.ts(7023)
T 52 text.html.markdown
L 
L         return z.optional(z.array(Activity)); //.optional();
T 60 text.html.markdown
L       },
T 8 text.html.markdown
L     });
T 7 text.html.markdown
L     ```
T 7 text.html.markdown
L 
L     Here are a couple rules of thumb:
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L     ### Object types only 
T 26 text.html.markdown
L     
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     Generally speaking, recursive inference only works with object types that are referencing each other. TypeScript has special handling for resolving getter-based recursive objects, which is what Zod relies on. If you try to add non-object types into the mix, you'll likely encounter errors.
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 293 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L     ```ts
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 9 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     const Activity = z.object({
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 31 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       name: z.string(),
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       get children() { // ❌ type error
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 40 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L         return z.optional(ActivityArray);
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 41 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       },
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     });
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 7 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L     const ActivityArray = z.array(Activity);
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 44 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     ```
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 7 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L     Sometimes you can get around this limitation by defining 
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 61 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L 
L     ### Avoid nesting function calls
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 36 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     Functions like `z.array()` and `z.optional()` accept Zod schemas, so when you use them TypeScript will do some type-checking on their inputs to make sure they are valid. But type checking is the enemy of recursive type inference—it's hard for TypeScript to *check* and *infer* types at the same time. Methods do not have this problem, so prefer methods over functions when possible (sorry Zod Mini users).
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 411 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L     ```ts
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 9 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     const Activity = z.object({
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 31 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       name: z.string(),
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       get subactivities() {
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 27 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L         // ^ ❌ 'subactivities' implicitly has return type 'any' because it does not 
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 86 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L         // have a return type annotation and is referenced directly or indirectly 
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 82 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L         // in one of its return expressions.ts(7023)
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 52 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L         return z.union([z.null(), Activity]);
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 45 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L       },
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 8 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     });
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 7 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L     ```
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
T 7 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown
L 
L     ### Fall back to type annotations on your getters
T 4 text.html.markdown markup.raw.block.markdown

L ## Unions
T 9 text.html.markdown
L 
L Union types (`A | B`) represent a logical "OR". Zod union schemas will check the input against each option in order. The first value that validates successfully is returned.
T 173 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const stringOrNumber = z.union([z.string(), z.number()]);
T 57 text.html.markdown
L // string | number
T 18 text.html.markdown
L 
L stringOrNumber.parse("foo"); // passes
T 38 text.html.markdown
L stringOrNumber.parse(14); // passes
T 35 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L ## Exclusive unions (XOR)
T 25 text.html.markdown
L 
L An exclusive union (XOR) is a union where exactly one option must match. Unlike regular unions that succeed when any option matches, `z.xor()` fails if zero options match OR if multiple options match.
T 200 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.xor([z.string(), z.number()]);
T 47 text.html.markdown
L 
L schema.parse("hello"); // ✅ passes
T 36 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse(42);      // ✅ passes
T 36 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse(true);    // ❌ fails (zero matches)
T 50 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This is useful when you want to ensure mutual exclusivity between options:
T 74 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L // Validate that exactly ONE of these matches
T 45 text.html.markdown
L const payment = z.xor([
T 23 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({ type: z.literal("card"), cardNumber: z.string() }),
T 64 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({ type: z.literal("bank"), accountNumber: z.string() }),
T 67 text.html.markdown
L ]);
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L payment.parse({ type: "card", cardNumber: "1234" }); // ✅ passes
T 66 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L If the input could match multiple options, `z.xor()` will fail. The resulting issue has `inclusive: false` and a `matches` array listing the indices of the options that matched:
T 177 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const overlapping = z.xor([z.string(), z.any()]);
T 49 text.html.markdown
L overlapping.parse("hello"); // ❌ fails (matches both string and any)
T 70 text.html.markdown
L // ZodError: Invalid input: more than one option matched
T 56 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Object options overlap more often than you'd expect, since `z.object()` strips unknown keys instead of rejecting them. Below, an input with both keys matches *both* options — the first strips `version`, the second keeps it. Use `z.strictObject()` (or `.strict()`) on the narrower option to make the branches mutually exclusive:
T 329 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const name = z.object({ name: z.string() });
T 44 text.html.markdown
L const version = name.extend({ version: z.string() });
T 53 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.xor([name, version]).parse({ name: "zod", version: "4" });           // ❌ fails (matches both)
T 98 text.html.markdown
L z.xor([name.strict(), version]).parse({ name: "zod", version: "4" });  // ✅ passes
T 84 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L 
L ## Discriminated unions
T 23 text.html.markdown
L 
L A [discriminated union](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/2/narrowing.html#discriminated-unions) is a special kind of union in which a) all the options are object schemas that b) share a particular key (the "discriminator"). Based on the value of the discriminator key, TypeScript is able to "narrow" the type signature as you'd expect.
T 347 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L type MyResult =
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   | { status: "success"; data: string }
T 39 text.html.markdown
L   | { status: "failed"; error: string };
T 40 text.html.markdown
L 
L function handleResult(result: MyResult){
T 40 text.html.markdown
L   if(result.status === "success"){
T 34 text.html.markdown
L     result.data; // string
T 26 text.html.markdown
L   } else {
T 10 text.html.markdown
L     result.error; // string
T 27 text.html.markdown
L   }
T 3 text.html.markdown
L }
T 1 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L You could represent it with a regular `z.union()`. But regular unions are *naive*—they check the input against each option in order and return the first one that passes. This can be slow for large unions.
T 206 text.html.markdown
L 
L So Zod provides a `z.discriminatedUnion()` API that uses a *discriminator key* to make parsing more efficient.
T 110 text.html.markdown
L 
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const MyResult = z.discriminatedUnion("status", [
T 49 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({ status: z.literal("success"), data: z.string() }),
T 63 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({ status: z.literal("failed"), error: z.string() }),
T 63 text.html.markdown
L ]);
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Each option should be an *object schema* whose discriminator prop (`status` in the example above) corresponds to some literal value or set of values, usually `z.enum()`, `z.literal()`, `z.null()`, or `z.undefined()`.
T 216 text.html.markdown
L 
L <Accordions type="single">
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 11 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 12 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 16 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.type.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 17 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.type.html punctuation.separator.key-value.html
T 18 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.type.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.begin.html
T 24 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.type.html string.quoted.double.html
T 25 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.type.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.end.html
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L <Accordion title="Nesting discriminated unions">
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 10 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 11 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 16 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 17 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html punctuation.separator.key-value.html
T 18 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.begin.html
T 46 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html
T 47 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.title.html string.quoted.double.html punctuation.definition.string.end.html
T 48 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L 
L   For advanced use cases, discriminated unions can be nested. Zod will figure out the optimal parsing strategy to leverage the discriminators at each level.
T 156 text.html.markdown
L 
L   ```ts
T 7 text.html.markdown
L   const BaseError = { status: z.literal("failed"), message: z.string() };
T 73 text.html.markdown
L   const MyErrors = z.discriminatedUnion("code", [
T 49 text.html.markdown
L     z.object({ ...BaseError, code: z.literal(400) }),
T 53 text.html.markdown
L     z.object({ ...BaseError, code: z.literal(401) }),
T 53 text.html.markdown
L     z.object({ ...BaseError, code: z.literal(500) }),
T 53 text.html.markdown
L   ]);
T 5 text.html.markdown
L 
L   const MyResult = z.discriminatedUnion("status", [
T 51 text.html.markdown
L     z.object({ status: z.literal("success"), data: z.string() }),
T 65 text.html.markdown
L     MyErrors
T 12 text.html.markdown
L   ]);
T 5 text.html.markdown
L   ```
T 5 text.html.markdown
L 
L </Accordion>

L ### `z.getDiscriminatedOption()`
T 32 text.html.markdown
L 
L To retrieve a single option by its discriminator value. The value must be one the union declares; anything else is a TypeScript error.
T 134 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Success = z.object({ status: z.literal("success"), data: z.string() });
T 77 text.html.markdown
L const Failed = z.object({ status: z.literal("failed"), error: z.string() });
T 76 text.html.markdown
L const MyResult = z.discriminatedUnion("status", [Success, Failed]);
T 67 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.getDiscriminatedOption(MyResult, "success"); // typeof Success
T 64 text.html.markdown
L z.getDiscriminatedOption(MyResult, "pending"); // ❌ not a declared discriminator
T 82 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The option is returned as-is, so `.shape` and the other object methods keep working.
T 84 text.html.markdown
L 
L If multiple options accept an absent discriminator, looking up `undefined` throws an ambiguity error. Their unique explicit tags remain retrievable.
T 148 text.html.markdown
L 
L ## Intersections
T 16 text.html.markdown

T 45 text.html.markdown

T 31 text.html.markdown
L

T 14 text.html.markdown

L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown

T 49 text.html.markdown
L type IdCache = z.infer<typeof IdCache>; // Record<string, string>
T 22 text.html.markdown

T 50 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html

T 15 text.html.markdown
L   carlotta: "77d2586b-9e8e-4ecf-8b21-ea7e0530eadd",
T 51 text.html.markdown

T 49 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The key schema can be any Zod schema that is assignable to `string | number | symbol`.
T 86 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown

T 59 text.html.markdown
L const AnyObject = z.record(Keys, z.unknown());
T 46 text.html.markdown
L // Record<string | number | symbol, unknown>
T 9 text.html.markdown
T 10 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 16 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html

T 44 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To create an object schemas containing keys defined by an enum:
T 63 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown

T 45 text.html.markdown
L const Person = z.record(Keys, z.string());
T 42 text.html.markdown
L // { id: string; name: string; email: string }
T 46 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Zod supports numeric keys inside records in a way that closely mirrors TypeScript itself. A `number` schema, when used as a record key, will validate that the key is a valid "numeric string". Additional numerical constraints (min, max, step, etc.) will also be validated.
T 271 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const numberKeys = z.record(z.number(), z.string());
T 52 text.html.markdown
L numberKeys.parse({ 
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   1: "one", // ✅
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   2: "two", // ✅
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   "1.5": "one", // ✅
T 22 text.html.markdown
L   "-3": "two", // ✅
T 21 text.html.markdown
L   abc: "one" // ❌
T 19 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L // further validation is also supported
T 39 text.html.markdown
L const intKeys = z.record(z.int().step(1).min(0).max(10), z.string());
T 69 text.html.markdown
L intKeys.parse({ 
T 16 text.html.markdown
L   0: "zero", // ✅
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   1: "one", // ✅
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   2: "two", // ✅
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   12: "twelve", // ❌
T 22 text.html.markdown
L   abc: "one" // ❌
T 19 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L A value schema that can stand in for an absent key — `.default()`, `.prefault()`, `.optional()` — makes that key optional on the input side, the same as it does inside `z.object()`:
T 185 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Person = z.record(z.enum(["id", "name"]), z.string().default(""));
T 72 text.html.markdown
L 
L type Input = z.input<typeof Person>; // { id?: string; name?: string }
T 20 text.html.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html

L ### `z.partialRecord`
T 21 text.html.markdown
L 
L 
L <Callout>
T 1 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 8 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L   If you pass a `z.enum` as the first argument to `z.record()`, Zod will exhaustively check that all enum values exist in the input as keys. This behavior agrees with TypeScript:
T 178 text.html.markdown
L 
L   ```ts
T 7 text.html.markdown
L   type MyRecord = Record<"a" | "b", string>;
T 44 text.html.markdown
L   const myRecord: MyRecord = { a: "foo", b: "bar" }; // ✅
T 59 text.html.markdown
L   const myRecord: MyRecord = { a: "foo" }; // ❌ missing required key `b`
T 74 text.html.markdown
L   ```
T 5 text.html.markdown
L 
L   For partial key sets, use `z.partialRecord()`.
T 48 text.html.markdown
L 
L </Callout>
T 2 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 9 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 10 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L 
L If you want a *partial* record type, use `z.partialRecord()`. This skips the special exhaustiveness checks Zod normally runs with `z.enum()` and `z.literal()` key schemas.
T 171 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Keys = z.enum(["id", "name", "email"]).or(z.never()); 
T 60 text.html.markdown
L const Person = z.partialRecord(Keys, z.string());
T 49 text.html.markdown
L // { id?: string; name?: string; email?: string }
T 49 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### `z.looseRecord`
T 19 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, `z.record()` errors on keys that don't match the key schema. Use `z.looseRecord()` to pass through non-matching keys unchanged. This is particularly useful when combined with intersections to model multiple pattern properties:
T 238 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z
T 16 text.html.markdown
L   .object({ name: z.string() })
T 31 text.html.markdown
L   .and(z.looseRecord(z.string().regex(/_phone$/), z.e164()));
T 61 text.html.markdown
L 
L type schema = z.infer<typeof schema>;
T 21 text.html.markdown
T 22 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 28 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 35 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.schema.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 36 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 37 text.html.markdown
L // => { name: string } & Record<string, string>
T 31 text.html.markdown
T 32 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 40 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 46 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.string.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 47 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L 
L schema.parse({ 
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   name: "John",
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   home_phone: "+12345678900",     // validated as phone number
T 62 text.html.markdown

T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L "Parsing" works a little differently with promise schemas. Validation happens in two parts:
T 91 text.html.markdown
L 
L 1. Zod synchronously checks that the input is an instance of Promise (i.e. an object with `.then` and `.catch` methods.).
T 121 text.html.markdown
L 2. Zod uses `.then` to attach an additional validation step onto the existing Promise. You'll have to use `.catch` on the returned Promise to handle validation failures.
T 169 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L numberPromise.parse("tuna");
T 28 text.html.markdown
L // ZodError: Non-Promise type: string
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L numberPromise.parse(Promise.resolve("tuna"));
T 45 text.html.markdown
L // => Promise<number>
T 13 text.html.markdown
T 14 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 20 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L 
L const test = async () => {
T 26 text.html.markdown
L   await numberPromise.parse(Promise.resolve("tuna"));
T 53 text.html.markdown
L   // ZodError: Non-number type: string
T 38 text.html.markdown
L

T 51 text.html.markdown
L   // => 3.14
T 12 text.html.markdown
L };
T 2 text.html.markdown
L ```

L ## Instanceof
T 13 text.html.markdown
L 
L You can use `z.instanceof` to check that the input is an instance of a class. This is useful to validate inputs against classes that are exported from third-party libraries.
T 173 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L class Test {
T 12 text.html.markdown
L   name: string;
T 15 text.html.markdown
L }
T 1 text.html.markdown
L 
L const TestSchema = z.instanceof(Test);
T 38 text.html.markdown
L 
L TestSchema.parse(new Test()); // ✅
T 36 text.html.markdown
L TestSchema.parse("whatever"); // ❌
T 36 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This works with built-in classes too.
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.instanceof(RegExp);
T 21 text.html.markdown
L z.instanceof(URL);
T 18 text.html.markdown
L z.instanceof(Error);
T 20 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Parsing returns the exact object that was passed in. Unlike `z.object()`, nothing is cloned, so the prototype, the unlisted properties, and the object identity all survive.
T 172 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const url = new URL("https://example.com");
T 43 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.instanceof(URL).parse(url) === url; // true
T 45 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L ### `z.property()` [#property]
T 30 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate a particular property of a class instance against a Zod schema:
T 75 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const blobSchema = z.instanceof(URL).check(
T 43 text.html.markdown
L   z.property("protocol", z.literal("https:", "Only HTTPS allowed"))
T 67 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L 
L blobSchema.parse(new URL("https://example.com")); // ✅
T 56 text.html.markdown
L blobSchema.parse(new URL("http://example.com")); // ❌
T 55 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The `z.property()` API works with any data type (but it's most useful when used in conjunction with `z.instanceof()`).
T 118 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const blobSchema = z.string().check(
T 36 text.html.markdown
L   z.property("length", z.number().min(10))
T 42 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L 
L blobSchema.parse("hello there!"); // ✅
T 40 text.html.markdown
L blobSchema.parse("hello."); // ❌
T 34 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L #### `when`
T 11 text.html.markdown
L 
L > **Note** — This is a power user feature and can absolutely be abused in ways that will increase the probability of uncaught errors originating from inside your refinements. 
T 177 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, refinements don't run if any *non-continuable* issues have already been encountered. Zod is careful to ensure the type signature of the value is correct before passing it into any refinement functions.
T 213 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.string().refine((val) => {
T 43 text.html.markdown
L   return val.length > 8
T 23 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L schema.parse(1234); // invalid_type: refinement won't be executed
T 65 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L In some cases, you want finer control over when refinements run. For instance consider this "password confirm" check:
T 117 text.html.markdown
L 
L <Tabs groupId="lib" items={["Zod", "Zod Mini"]}>

L The `.refine()` API is syntactic sugar atop a more versatile (and verbose) API called `.check()`. You can use this API to create multiple issues in a single refinement or have full control of the generated issue objects.

L ## Codecs
T 9 text.html.markdown
L 
L > Introduced in `zod@4.1`. Refer to the dedicated [Codecs](/codecs) page for more information.
T 94 text.html.markdown
L 
L Codecs are a special kind of schema that implement *bidirectional transformations* between two other schemas.
T 109 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const stringToDate = z.codec(
T 29 text.html.markdown
L   z.iso.datetime(),  // input schema: ISO date string
T 53 text.html.markdown
L   z.date(),          // output schema: Date object
T 50 text.html.markdown
L   {
T 3 text.html.markdown
L     decode: (isoString) => new Date(isoString), // ISO string → Date
T 70 text.html.markdown
L     encode: (date) => date.toISOString(),       // Date → ISO string
T 70 text.html.markdown
L   }
T 3 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L A regular `.parse()` operations performs the *forward transform*. It calls the codec's `decode` function.
T 105 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L stringToDate.parse("2024-01-15T10:30:00.000Z"); // => Date
T 58 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L You can alternatively use the top-level `z.decode()` function. Unlike `.parse()` (which accepts `unknown` input), `z.decode()` expects a strongly-typed input (`string` in this example).
T 185 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.decode(stringToDate, "2024-01-15T10:30:00.000Z"); // => Date
T 62 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To perform the *reverse transform*, use the inverse: `z.encode()`.
T 66 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.encode(stringToDate, new Date("2024-01-15")); // => "2024-01-15T00:00:00.000Z"
T 80 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Use `z.invertCodec()` to derive a new codec with the input and output schemas swapped.
T 86 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const dateToString = z.invertCodec(stringToDate);
T 49 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.decode(dateToString, new Date("2024-01-15")); // => string
T 60 text.html.markdown
L z.encode(dateToString, "2024-01-15T00:00:00.000Z"); // => Date
T 62 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Refer to the dedicated [Codecs](/codecs) page for more information. That page contains implementations for commonly-needed codecs that you can copy/paste into your project:
T 172 text.html.markdown
L 
L - [**`stringToNumber`**](/codecs#stringtonumber)
T 48 text.html.markdown
L - [**`stringToInt`**](/codecs#stringtoint)
T 42 text.html.markdown
L - [**`stringToBigInt`**](/codecs#stringtobigint)
T 48 text.html.markdown
L - [**`numberToBigInt`**](/codecs#numbertobigint)
T 48 text.html.markdown
L - [**`isoDatetimeToDate`**](/codecs#isodatetimetodate)
T 54 text.html.markdown
L - [**`epochSecondsToDate`**](/codecs#epochsecondstodate)
T 56 text.html.markdown
L - [**`epochMillisToDate`**](/codecs#epochmillistodate)
T 54 text.html.markdown
L - [**`jsonCodec`**](/codecs#jsoncodec)
T 38 text.html.markdown
L - [**`utf8ToBytes`**](/codecs#utf8tobytes)
T 42 text.html.markdown
L - [**`bytesToUtf8`**](/codecs#bytestoutf8)
T 42 text.html.markdown
L - [**`base64ToBytes`**](/codecs#base64tobytes)
T 46 text.html.markdown
L - [**`base64urlToBytes`**](/codecs#base64urltobytes)
T 52 text.html.markdown
L - [**`hexToBytes`**](/codecs#hextobytes)
T 40 text.html.markdown
L - [**`stringToURL`**](/codecs#stringtourl)
T 42 text.html.markdown
L - [**`stringToHttpURL`**](/codecs#stringtohttpurl)
T 50 text.html.markdown
L - [**`uriComponent`**](/codecs#uricomponent)
T 44 text.html.markdown
L - [**`stringToBoolean`**](/codecs#stringtoboolean)
T 50 text.html.markdown

L ### `z.input()` and `z.output()` [#input-output]
T 48 text.html.markdown
L 
L Runtime counterparts to the type-level `z.input<T>` / `z.output<T>`: they replace every pipe in a schema with its input or output side. Useful for reaching [codecs](/codecs) nested inside objects, records, or maps, where `.in` / `.out` can't.
T 47 text.html.markdown
T 48 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 49 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 50 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 63 text.html.markdown
T 64 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 65 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 66 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 242 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Event = z.object({ name: z.string(), at: stringToDate });
T 63 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.input(Event).parse({ name: "launch", at: "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z" }); // ✅
T 76 text.html.markdown
L z.output(Event).parse({ name: "launch", at: new Date() });            // ✅
T 76 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Only codecs have two real sides. `z.output()` on a one-way [transform](#transforms) returns the transform, which validates nothing; `z.input()` on a `z.preprocess()` returns the schema the preprocessor feeds.
T 208 text.html.markdown
L 
L A wrapper's stored value survives only on the side it belongs to, so with a codec underneath, `z.input()` drops a `.default()` or `.catch()` and `z.output()` drops a `.prefault()`.
T 180 text.html.markdown
L

L 
L To perform validation logic inside a transform, use `ctx`. To report a validation issue, push a new issue onto `ctx.issues` (similar to the [`.check()`](#check) API).
T 166 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const coercedInt = z.transform((val, ctx) => {
T 46 text.html.markdown
L   try {
T 7 text.html.markdown
L     const parsed = Number.parseInt(String(val));
T 48 text.html.markdown
L     return parsed;
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   } catch (e) {
T 15 text.html.markdown
L     ctx.issues.push({
T 21 text.html.markdown
L       code: "custom",
T 21 text.html.markdown
L       message: "Not a number",
T 30 text.html.markdown
L       input: val,
T 17 text.html.markdown
L     });
T 7 text.html.markdown
L 
L     // this is a special constant with type `never`
T 51 text.html.markdown
L     // returning it lets you exit the transform without impacting the inferred return type
T 90 text.html.markdown
L     return z.NEVER;
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   }
T 3 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Most commonly, transforms are used in conjunction with [Pipes](#pipes). This combination is useful for performing some initial validation, then transforming the parsed data into another form.

L ### `.preprocess()` 
T 20 text.html.markdown
L 
L Piping a transform into another schema is another common pattern, so Zod provides a convenience `z.preprocess()` function.
T 122 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const coercedInt = z.preprocess((val) => {
T 42 text.html.markdown
L   if (typeof val === "string") {
T 32 text.html.markdown
L     return Number.parseInt(val);
T 32 text.html.markdown
L   }
T 3 text.html.markdown
L   return val;
T 13 text.html.markdown
L }, z.int());
T 12 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, the input type of a `z.preprocess()` schema is `unknown`, since the preprocessor is expected to handle arbitrary input. To narrow the input type, annotate the preprocessor's parameter directly:
T 205 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const trimmed = z.preprocess(
T 29 text.html.markdown
L   (val: string | null | undefined) => val?.trim() ?? "",
T 56 text.html.markdown
L   z.string()
T 12 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L 
L type Input = z.input<typeof trimmed>;  // string | null | undefined
T 20 text.html.markdown
T 21 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 27 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 28 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 35 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.trimmed.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 36 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 67 text.html.markdown
L type Output = z.output<typeof trimmed>; // string
T 22 text.html.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 37 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.trimmed.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 38 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 49 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This is useful when integrating with libraries like `react-hook-form` that derive their form value type from `z.input<>`.
T 121 text.html.markdown

L ## Prefaults
T 12 text.html.markdown
L 
L In Zod, setting a *default* value will short-circuit the parsing process. If the input is `undefined`, the default value is eagerly returned. As such, the default value must be assignable to the *output type* of the schema.
T 223 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.string().transform(val => val.length).default(0);
T 66 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse(undefined); // => 0
T 32 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Sometimes, it's useful to define a *prefault* ("pre-parse default") value. If the input is `undefined`, the prefault value will be parsed instead. The parsing process is *not* short circuited. As such, the prefault value must be assignable to the *input type* of the schema.
T 274 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const schema = z.string().transform(val => val.length).prefault("tuna");
T 72 text.html.markdown
L schema.parse(undefined); // => 4
T 32 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This is also useful if you want to pass some input value through some mutating refinements.
T 91 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const a = z.string().trim().toUpperCase().prefault("  tuna  ");
T 63 text.html.markdown
L a.parse(undefined); // => "TUNA"
T 32 text.html.markdown
L 
L const b = z.string().trim().toUpperCase().default("  tuna  ");
T 62 text.html.markdown
L b.parse(undefined); // => "  tuna  "
T 36 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L ## Branded types
T 16 text.html.markdown
L 
L TypeScript's type system is [structural](https://www.typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/type-compatibility.html), meaning that two types that are structurally equivalent are considered the same.
T 192 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L type Cat = { name: string };
T 28 text.html.markdown
L type Dog = { name: string };
T 28 text.html.markdown
L 
L const pluto: Dog = { name: "pluto" };
T 37 text.html.markdown
L const simba: Cat = pluto; // works fine
T 39 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L In some cases, it can be desirable to simulate [nominal typing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nominal_type_system) inside TypeScript. This can be achieved with *branded types* (also known as "opaque types").
T 207 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Cat = z.object({ name: z.string() }).brand<"Cat">();
T 58 text.html.markdown
L const Dog = z.object({ name: z.string() }).brand<"Dog">();
T 58 text.html.markdown
L 
L type Cat = z.infer<typeof Cat>; // { name: string } & z.$brand<"Cat">
T 18 text.html.markdown
T 19 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 25 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.Cat.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 69 text.html.markdown
L type Dog = z.infer<typeof Dog>; // { name: string } & z.$brand<"Dog">
T 18 text.html.markdown
T 19 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 25 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 29 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.Dog.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 69 text.html.markdown
L 
L const pluto = Dog.parse({ name: "pluto" });
T 43 text.html.markdown
L const simba: Cat = pluto; // ❌ not allowed
T 44 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Under the hood, this works by attaching a "brand" to the schema's inferred type.
T 80 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const Cat = z.object({ name: z.string() }).brand<"Cat">();
T 58 text.html.markdown
L type Cat = z.output<typeof Cat>; // { name: string } & z.$brand<"Cat">
T 19 text.html.markdown
T 20 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 27 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.Cat.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 70 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L With this brand, plain (unbranded) data structures are not assignable to the inferred type. You have to parse some data with the schema to get branded data.
T 156 text.html.markdown
L 
L > Note that branded types do not affect the runtime result of `.parse`. It is a static-only construct.
T 102 text.html.markdown
L 
L By default, only the *output type* is branded. 
T 47 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const USD = z.string().brand<"USD">();
T 38 text.html.markdown
L 
L type USDOutput = z.output<typeof USD>; // string & z.$brand<"USD">
T 25 text.html.markdown
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 32 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 33 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 36 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.USD.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 37 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 66 text.html.markdown
L type USDInput = z.input<typeof USD>; // string
T 23 text.html.markdown
T 24 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 34 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.USD.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 35 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 46 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L To customize this, pass a second generic to `.brand()` to specify the direction of the brand.
T 93 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L // requires Zod 4.2+
T 20 text.html.markdown
L z.string().brand<"Cat", "out">(); // output is branded (default)
T 64 text.html.markdown
L z.string().brand<"Cat", "in">(); // input is branded
T 52 text.html.markdown
L z.string().brand<"Cat", "inout">(); // both are branded
T 55 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ## JSON
T 7 text.html.markdown
L 
L To validate any JSON-encodable value:
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const jsonSchema = z.json();
T 28 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This is a convenience API that returns the following union schema:
T 66 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const jsonSchema = z.lazy(() => {
T 33 text.html.markdown
L   return z.union([
T 18 text.html.markdown
L     z.string(params), 
T 22 text.html.markdown
L     z.number(), 
T 16 text.html.markdown
L     z.boolean(), 
T 17 text.html.markdown
L     z.null(), 
T 14 text.html.markdown
L     z.array(jsonSchema), 
T 25 text.html.markdown
L     z.record(z.string(), jsonSchema)
T 36 text.html.markdown
L   ]);
T 5 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L
