L ## Functions
T 12 text.html.markdown
L 
L Zod provides a `z.function()` utility for defining Zod-validated functions. This way, you can avoid intermixing validation code with your business logic.
T 153 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const MyFunction = z.function({
T 31 text.html.markdown
L   input: [z.string()], // parameters (must be an array or a ZodTuple)
T 69 text.html.markdown
L   output: z.number()  // return type
T 36 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L type MyFunction = z.infer<typeof MyFunction>;
T 25 text.html.markdown
T 26 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 32 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 33 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative
T 43 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative meta.attribute.unrecognized.MyFunction.html entity.other.attribute-name.html
T 44 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 45 text.html.markdown
L // (input: string) => number
T 28 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L Function schemas have an `.implement()` method which accepts a function and returns a new function that automatically validates its inputs and outputs.
T 151 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const computeTrimmedLength = MyFunction.implement((input) => {
T 62 text.html.markdown
L   // TypeScript knows input is a string!
T 40 text.html.markdown
L   return input.trim().length;
T 29 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L computeTrimmedLength("sandwich"); // => 8
T 41 text.html.markdown
L computeTrimmedLength(" asdf "); // => 4
T 39 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L This function will throw a `ZodError` if the input is invalid:
T 62 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L computeTrimmedLength(42); // throws ZodError
T 44 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L If you only care about validating inputs, you can omit the `output` field.
T 74 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const MyFunction = z.function({
T 31 text.html.markdown
L   input: [z.string()], // parameters (must be an array or a ZodTuple)
T 69 text.html.markdown
L });
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L const computeTrimmedLength = MyFunction.implement((input) => input.trim.length);
T 80 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L 
L Use the `.implementAsync()` method to create an async function.
T 63 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L const computeTrimmedLengthAsync = MyFunction.implementAsync(
T 60 text.html.markdown
L   async (input) => input.trim().length
T 38 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L 
L computeTrimmedLengthAsync("sandwich"); // => Promise<8>
T 52 text.html.markdown
T 53 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 54 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 55 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown

L ## Custom
T 9 text.html.markdown
L 
L You can create a Zod schema for any TypeScript type by using `z.custom()`. This is useful for validating types from third-party libraries or any other type that isn't covered by a built-in schema. For class instances, prefer [`z.instanceof()`](#instanceof); for template literal types, prefer [`z.templateLiteral()`](#template-literals).
T 337 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L import { Decimal } from "decimal.js";
T 37 text.html.markdown
L 
L const decimalSchema = z.custom<Decimal>((val) => Decimal.isDecimal(val));
T 30 text.html.markdown
T 31 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 38 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 39 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 73 text.html.markdown
L 
L decimalSchema.parse(new Decimal("1.5")); // passes
T 50 text.html.markdown
L decimalSchema.parse("1.5");              // throws
T 50 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L If you don't provide a validation function, Zod will allow any value. This can be dangerous!
T 92 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.custom<{ arg: string }>(); // performs no validation
T 54 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L You can customize the error message and other options by passing a second argument. This parameter works the same way as the params parameter of [`.refine`](#refine).
T 166 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.custom<...>((val) => ..., "custom error message");
T 52 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L

L ## Matching an existing type
T 28 text.html.markdown
L 
L Sometimes the type comes first: a database model, a type from a generated client, an interface you don't own. Pass it to `z.toZod<T>()` and TypeScript checks that the schema's output type is exactly `T`.
T 129 text.html.markdown
T 130 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 131 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 132 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 203 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L type Player = {
T 15 text.html.markdown
L   username: string;
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   xp: number;
T 13 text.html.markdown
L };
T 2 text.html.markdown
L 
L const Player = z.toZod<Player>()(
T 22 text.html.markdown
T 23 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html

T 30 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 33 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({
T 12 text.html.markdown
L     username: z.string(),
T 25 text.html.markdown
L     xp: z.number(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L   })
T 4 text.html.markdown
L );

T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The check is exact type equality, so any drift from the type is a compile error:
T 80 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L z.toZod<Player>()(
T 7 text.html.markdown
T 8 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 14 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 15 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 18 text.html.markdown
L   z.object({
T 12 text.html.markdown
L     username: z.string(),
T 25 text.html.markdown
L     xp: z.number(),
T 19 text.html.markdown
L     admin: z.boolean(), // ❌ extra key
T 40 text.html.markdown
L   })
T 4 text.html.markdown
L );
T 2 text.html.markdown
L ```
T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L The usual alternative is `satisfies z.ZodType<Player>`, which only checks assignability. It catches a missing required key, but extra keys, omitted optional keys, and a bare `z.any()` all slip through:
T 45 text.html.markdown
T 46 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.begin.html
T 52 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative entity.name.tag.html
T 53 text.html.markdown meta.tag.other.unrecognized.html.derivative punctuation.definition.tag.end.html
T 201 text.html.markdown
L

T 3 text.html.markdown
L 
L An enum target matches `z.enum(...)`, and stays nominal — a plain literal union doesn't satisfy it:
T 101 text.html.markdown
L 
L ```ts
T 5 text.html.markdown
L enum Level {
T 12 text.html.markdown
L   Noob = "noob",
T 16 text.html.markdown
L   Pro = "pro",
T 14 text.html.markdown
L }
T 1 text.html.markdown
L 
L z.toZod<{ level: Level }>()(z.object({ level: z.enum(Level) })); // ✅
T 71 text.html.markdown
L z.toZod<{ level: Level }>()(z.object({ level: z.enum(["noob", "pro"]) })); // ❌
T 81 text.html.markdown
L ```
